## 2026-03-29 15:34 MDT

- The inherited shared-file edit stop is live, and it exposed the next narrower worker seam immediately on SamBot task `180`.
- Fresh live evidence after `bafdcb70`: task `180` recovery turns `39eb8c70-1620-4fb7-b0a8-caaa6784ab69` and `4a9cb469-a7c5-41e1-96e0-a6f0a0e9cb36` both ended `validation_loop_blocked` with `[Task shared-deliverable guard blocked a decomposed child lane from replacing the inherited parent file with file.write ...]`, but the worker still allowed the same active execution to rearm another `task_recovery_resume`.
- Root cause: the worker's terminal blocked-stop suppression for recovery resumes only applied on the abandoned/rejected execution repair path and stale terminal recovery requeue path. - Added focused integration coverage in [`internal/jobqueue/worker_integration_test.go`](/Users/sam/dev/otter-camp/internal/jobqueue/worker_integration_test.go) for both worker entry points:

## 2026-03-29 15:09 MDT

- Picked up the next narrow task-lane hardening seam from fresh SamBot child sessions `bfb35b67-7f32-434d-8192-91211488b6ea` (task `180`) and `bfb45b88-a66f-4af5-8ef2-7ab208628d47` (task `181`).
- Fresh live evidence on `repo_version=3608`: both lanes made durable `file.edit` progress against the inherited parent deliverable `planning/sambot-architecture.md`, but the engine did not count that as enough to stop the turn. Task `180` in particular edited the file successfully, then spent more tool calls on `head` / `grep` / `wc` verification and finally hit blocked `git.commit` handoff with `task_git_commit_blocked`.
- Root cause: `shouldStopAfterExecutionDeliverableWrite(...)` only treated explicit task deliverables, artifact writes, and recovery checkpoint targets as proof-bearing mutations. Decomposed child tasks that inherit a shared single-file parent deliverable were falling through even after a successful in-place edit to that inherited file.
- Patched [`internal/turn/engine.go`](/Users/sam/dev/otter-camp/internal/turn/engine.go) so the same turn-level stop path now also recognizes a successful `file.edit` on the inherited shared single-file deliverable path resolved from the decomposition parent. Added focused regression coverage in [`internal/turn/engine_test.go`](/Users/sam/dev/otter-camp/internal/turn/engine_test.go) with `TestShouldStopAfterExecutionDeliverableWriteStopsForInheritedSharedSingleFileEdit`, and verified with `GOFLAGS='' go test ./internal/turn -run 'Test(ShouldStopAfterExecutionArtifactWriteForExplicitDirectoryOutput|ShouldStopAfterExecutionArtifactWriteIgnoresUndeclaredPath|ShouldStopAfterExecutionDeliverableWriteStopsForRecoveryCheckpointTarget|ShouldStopAfterExecutionDeliverableWriteStopsForInheritedSharedSingleFileEdit)$' -count=1`.
- Post-deploy live proof on `repo_version=3609`: SamBot task `180` turn `68f64ed4-80d8-4ba1-8ec2-378cca571612` still paid the earlier `git.status` / `git.diff` / blocked `git.commit` sequence, but once it emitted `file.edit planning/sambot-architecture.md -> replacements_made=1`, the turn completed with no further tool calls after that inherited shared-file edit.
- That means this slice is behaving as implemented, and the next narrower hardening seam is now explicit: stop task lanes from attempting git-based verification/handoff once the inherited shared deliverable is already dirty and clearly remains task-owned runtime completion work.
